1. ﻿//*********************************************************
2. //
3. // Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.
4. // This code is licensed under the MIT License (MIT).
5. // THIS CODE IS PROVIDED *AS IS* WITHOUT WARRANTY OF
6. // ANY KIND, EITHER EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING ANY
7. // IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR
8. // PURPOSE, MERCHANTABILITY, OR NON-INFRINGEMENT.
9. //
10. //*********************************************************
11. 
12. using System.Collections.Generic;
13. using Windows.Graphics.Imaging;
14. using Windows.Storage.FileProperties;
15. 
16. namespace SimpleImaging
17. {
18.     /// <summary>
19.     /// Contains helper functionality, including handling/converting EXIF orientation values.
20.     /// </summary>
21.     public static class Helpers
22.     {
23.         /// <summary>
24.         /// Retrieves all of the file extensions supported by the bitmap codecs on the system,
25.         /// and inserts them into the provided fileTypeFilter parameter.
26.         /// </summary>
27.         /// <param name="fileTypeFilter">FileOpenPicker.FileTypeFilter member</param>
28.         public static void FillDecoderExtensions(IList<string> fileTypeFilter)
29.         {
30.             IReadOnlyList<BitmapCodecInformation> codecInfoList =
31.                 BitmapDecoder.GetDecoderInformationEnumerator();
32. 
33.             foreach (BitmapCodecInformation decoderInfo in codecInfoList)
34.             {
35.                 // Each bitmap codec contains a list of file extensions it supports; add each
36.                 // list item to fileTypeFilter.
37.                 foreach (string extension in decoderInfo.FileExtensions)
38.                 {
39.                     fileTypeFilter.Add(extension);
40.                 }
41.             }
42.         }
43. 
44.         /// <summary>
45.         /// Converts a PhotoOrientation value into a human-readable string.
46.         /// The text is adapted from the EXIF specification.
47.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention,
48.         /// while the EXIF spec uses a clockwise convention.
49.         /// </summary>
50.         public static string GetOrientationString(PhotoOrientation input)
51.         {
52.             switch (input)
53.             {
54.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
55.                     return "No rotation";
56.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipHorizontal:
57.                     return "Flip horizontally";
58.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
59.                     return "Rotate 180˚ clockwise";
60.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipVertical:
61.                     return "Flip vertically";
62.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transpose:
63.                     return "Rotate 270˚ clockwise, then flip horizontally";
64.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
65.                     return "Rotate 90˚ clockwise";
66.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transverse:
67.                     return "Rotate 90˚ clockwise, then flip horizontally";
68.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
69.                     return "Rotate 270˚ clockwise";
70.                 case PhotoOrientation.Unspecified:
71.                 default:
72.                     return "Unspecified";
73.             }
74.         }
75. 
76.         /// <summary>
77.         /// Converts a Windows.Storage.FileProperties.PhotoOrientation value into a
78.         /// Windows.Graphics.Imaging.BitmapRotation value.
79.         /// For PhotoOrientation values reflecting a flip/mirroring operation, returns "None";
80.         /// therefore this is a potentially lossy transformation.
81.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention,
82.         /// while BitmapRotation uses a clockwise convention.
83.         /// </summary>
84.         public static BitmapRotation ConvertToBitmapRotation(PhotoOrientation input)
85.         {
86.             switch (input)
87.             {
88.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
89.                     return BitmapRotation.None;
90.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
91.                     return BitmapRotation.Clockwise90Degrees;
92.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
93.                     return BitmapRotation.Clockwise180Degrees;
94.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
95.                     return BitmapRotation.Clockwise270Degrees;
96.                 default:
97.                     // Ignore any flip/mirrored values.
98.                     return BitmapRotation.None;
99.             }
100.         }
101. 
102.         /// <summary>
103.         /// Converts a Windows.Graphics.Imaging.BitmapRotation value into a
104.         /// Windows.Storage.FileProperties.PhotoOrientation value.
105.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention,
106.         /// while BitmapRotation uses a clockwise convention.
107.         /// </summary>
108.         public static PhotoOrientation ConvertToPhotoOrientation(BitmapRotation input)
109.         {
110.             switch (input)
111.             {
112.                 case BitmapRotation.None:
113.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
114.                 case BitmapRotation.Clockwise90Degrees:
115.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
116.                 case BitmapRotation.Clockwise180Degrees:
117.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
118.                 case BitmapRotation.Clockwise270Degrees:
119.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
120.                 default:
121.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
122.             }
123.         }
124. 
125.         /// <summary>
126.         /// Converts an unsigned integer, corresponding to the EXIF orientation flag, to a
127.         /// Windows.Storage.FileProperties.PhotoOrientation value. Note that the actual
128.         /// PhotoOrientation enumeration values directly map to the EXIF orientation flag; 
129.         /// this method simply provides a typesafe means of converting between the two in C#,
130.         /// in addition to handling the Unspecified case.
131.         /// </summary>
132.         public static PhotoOrientation ConvertToPhotoOrientation(ushort input)
133.         {
134.             switch (input)
135.             {
136.                 case 1:
137.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
138.                 case 2:
139.                     return PhotoOrientation.FlipHorizontal;
140.                 case 3:
141.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
142.                 case 4:
143.                     return PhotoOrientation.FlipVertical;
144.                 case 5:
145.                     return PhotoOrientation.Transpose;
146.                 case 6:
147.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
148.                 case 7:
149.                     return PhotoOrientation.Transverse;
150.                 case 8:
151.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
152.                 default:
153.                     return PhotoOrientation.Unspecified;
154.             }
155.         }
156. 
157.         /// <summary>
158.         /// Counterpart to ConvertToPhotoOrientation(ushort input), maps PhotoOrientation enum
159.         /// values to an unsigned 16-bit integer representing the EXIF orientation flag.
160.         /// </summary>
161.         public static ushort ConvertToExifOrientationFlag(PhotoOrientation input)
162.         {
163.             switch (input)
164.             {
165.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
166.                     return 1;
167.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipHorizontal:
168.                     return 2;
169.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
170.                     return 3;
171.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipVertical:
172.                     return 4;
173.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transpose:
174.                     return 5;
175.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
176.                     return 6;
177.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transverse:
178.                     return 7;
179.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
180.                     return 8;
181.                 default:
182.                     return 1;
183.             }
184.         }
185. 
186.         /// <summary>
187.         /// "Adds" two PhotoOrientation values. For simplicity, does not handle any values with
188.         /// flip/mirroring; therefore this is a potentially lossy transformation.
189.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention.
190.         /// </summary>
191.         public static PhotoOrientation AddPhotoOrientation(
192.             PhotoOrientation value1,
193.             PhotoOrientation value2
194.             )
195.         {
196.             switch (value2)
197.             {
198.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
199.                     return Add90DegreesCCW(value1);
200.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
201.                     return Add90DegreesCCW(Add90DegreesCCW(value1));
202.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
203.                     return Add90DegreesCW(value1);
204.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
205.                 default:
206.                     // Ignore any values with flip/mirroring.
207.                     return value1;
208.             }
209.         }
210. 
211.         /// <summary>
212.         /// "Add" 90 degrees clockwise rotation to a PhotoOrientation value.
213.         /// For simplicity, does not handle any values with flip/mirroring; therefore this can be
214.         /// a lossy transformation.
215.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention.
216.         /// </summary>
217.         public static PhotoOrientation Add90DegreesCW(PhotoOrientation input)
218.         {
219.             switch (input)
220.             {
221.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
222.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
223.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
224.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
225.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
226.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
227.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
228.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
229.                 default:
230.                     // Ignore any values with flip/mirroring.
231.                     return PhotoOrientation.Unspecified;
232.             }
233.         }
234. 
235.         /// <summary>
236.         /// "Add" 90 degrees counter-clockwise rotation to a PhotoOrientation value.
237.         /// For simplicity, does not handle any values with flip/mirroring; therefore this can be
238.         /// a lossy transformation.
239.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention.
240.         /// </summary>
241.         public static PhotoOrientation Add90DegreesCCW(PhotoOrientation input)
242.         {
243.             switch (input)
244.             {
245.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
246.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
247.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
248.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
249.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
250.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
251.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
252.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
253.                 default:
254.                     // Ignore any values with flip/mirroring.
255.                     return PhotoOrientation.Unspecified;
256.             }
257.         }

<a id="pickers"></a>
## Pickers and Win32 SurfacesUWP pickers infer the active window. WinUI 3 desktop pickers must be initialized explicitly or `ShowAsync` throws `COMException`.```csharp
var picker = new FileOpenPicker();
picker.FileTypeFilter.Add(".txt");
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
WinRT.Interop.InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(picker, hwnd);
var file = await picker.PickSingleFileAsync();
```Apply the same `InitializeWithWindow.Initialize(obj, hwnd)` pattern to `FolderPicker`, `FileSavePicker`, `DataTransferManager` (Share), `PrintManager`, and other UI surfaces that target a window.
<exited with exit code 0>

12. using System;
13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using Windows.UI.Core;
15. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
16. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers;
17. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;

20. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Navigation;

22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
23. 
24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }

Remove them for a plain opaque window:\n\n**MainWindow.xaml** — remove `<Window.SystemBackdrop><MicaBackdrop /></Window.SystemBackdrop>` and the `<TitleBar>` block, leaving a simple frame:\n```xml\n<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"utf-8\" ?>\n<Window\n    x:Class=\"SimpleImaging.MainWindow\"\n    xmlns=\"http://schemas.microsoft.com/winfx/2006/xaml/presentation\"\n    xmlns:x=\"http://schemas.microsoft.com/winfx/2006/xaml\"\n    xmlns:local=\"using:SimpleImaging\"\n    Title=\"SimpleImaging\">\n    <Frame x:Name=\"RootFrame\" />\n</Window>\n```\n\n**MainWindow.xaml.cs** — remove `ExtendsContentIntoTitleBar = true;` and `SetTitleBar(AppTitleBar);`:\n```csharp\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml;\n\nnamespace SimpleImaging;\n\npublic sealed partial class MainWindow : Window\n{\n    public static MainWindow Current { get; private set; }\n\n    public MainWindow()\n    {\n        Current = this;\n        InitializeComponent();\n        AppWindow.SetIcon(\"Assets/AppIcon.ico\");\n        RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Fix MainPage.xaml and MainPage.xaml.cs\nRewrite these to implement the SDKTemplate shell pattern (scenario list + navigation frame + status bar). Use `NavigationView` style but keep it close to the original SplitView pattern.\n\n**CRITICAL**: The `SampleConfiguration.cs` has the scenarios list, uses namespace `SDKTemplate` for `MainPage` partial class. The `Scenario1.xaml.cs` and `Scenario2.xaml.cs` reference `MainPage.Current` (type `SDKTemplate.MainPage`) and call `rootPage.NotifyUser(...)`.\n\nThe `SampleConfiguration.cs` file already }\n}\n```\n\nSo `MainPage` must be in `namespace SDKTemplate` and the XAML `x:Class` must be `SDKTemplate.MainPage`.\n\n**MainPage.xaml** — replace the scaffold blank grid with the SDKTemplate Replace `Window.Current.Bounds.Width` with `App.MainWindow.Bounds.Width` (or use AppWindow)\n- Replace `Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess` with `DispatcherQueue.HasThreadAccess`\n- Replace `Dispatcher.RunAsync(...)` with `DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(...)`\n- Replace `Windows.UI.Colors.Green` with `Microsoft.UI.Colors` (or use SolidColorBrush differently since Microsoft.UI.Colors doesn't exist — use hardcoded colors from Windows.UI.Colors which still works in WinUI 3 via the Platform SDK, or use Color from Windows.UI)\n- Replace `FrameworkElementAutomationPeer` — still available in WinUI 3 via `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers`\n- Replace `Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync` — same API still works in WinUI 3\n\nHere's the corrected MainPage.xaml.cs:\n```csharp\n//*********************************************************\n//\n// Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.\n// This code is licensed under the MIT License (MIT).\n// THIS CODE IS PROVIDED *AS IS* WITHOUT WARRANTY OF\n// ANY KIND, EITHER EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING ANY\n// IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR\n// PURPOSE, MERCHANTABILITY, OR NON-INFRINGEMENT.\n//\n//*********************************************************\n\nusing Microsoft.UI;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;\nusing SimpleImaging;\nusing System;\nusing System.Collections.Generic;\n\nnamespace SDKTemplate\n{\n    /// <summary>\n    /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.\n    /// </summary>\n    public sealed partial class MainPage : Page\n    {\n        public static MainPage Current;\n\n        public MainPage()\n        {\n            this.InitializeComponent();\n            Current = this;\n            SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;\n        }\n\n        protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)\n        {\n            var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();\n            int i = 1;\n            foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)\n            {\n                itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $\"{i++}) {s.Title}\", ClassType = s.ClassType });\n            }\n            ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;\n            ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;\n        }\n\n        private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);\n            ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;\n            Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;\n            if (s != null)\n            {\n                ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);\n            }\n        }\n\n        public List<Scenario> Scenarios\n        {\n            get { return this.scenarios; }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Display a message to the user.\n        /// This method may be called from any thread.\n        /// </summary>\n        public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)\n        {\n            if (DispatcherQueue.HasThreadAccess)\n            {\n                UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)\n        {\n            switch (type)\n            {\n                case NotifyType.StatusMessage:\n                    StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Green);\n                    break;\n                case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:\n                    StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);\n                    break;\n            }\n\n            StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;\n\n            if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)\n            {\n                StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;\n                StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;\n                StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;\n            }\n\n            var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);\n            if (peer != null)\n            {\n                peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);\n            }\n        }\n\n        async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));\n        }\n\n        private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public enum NotifyType\n    {\n        StatusMessage,\n        ErrorMessage\n    };\n}\n```\n\n### 3. The App.xaml.cs needs to expose `MainWindow`:\n\nLook at the current App.xaml.cs and add `public static MainWindow MainWindow { get; private set; }` and set it when creating the window.\n\n### 4. 11. 
12. using System.Collections.Generic;
13. using Windows.Graphics.Imaging;
14. using Windows.Storage.FileProperties;
15. 
16. namespace SimpleImaging
17. {
18.     /// <summary>
19.     /// Contains helper functionality, including handling/converting EXIF orientation values.
20.     /// </summary>
21.     public static class Helpers
22.     {
23.         /// <summary>
24.         /// Retrieves all of the file extensions supported by the bitmap codecs on the system,
25.         /// and inserts them into the provided fileTypeFilter parameter.
26.         /// </summary>
27.         /// <param name="fileTypeFilter">FileOpenPicker.FileTypeFilter member</param>
28.         public static void FillDecoderExtensions(IList<string> fileTypeFilter)
29.         {
30.             IReadOnlyList<BitmapCodecInformation> codecInfoList =
31.                 BitmapDecoder.GetDecoderInformationEnumerator();
32. 
33.             foreach (BitmapCodecInformation decoderInfo in codecInfoList)
34.             {
35.                 // Each bitmap codec contains a list of file extensions it supports; add each
36.                 // list item to fileTypeFilter.
37.                 foreach (string extension in decoderInfo.FileExtensions)
38.                 {
39.                     fileTypeFilter.Add(extension);
40.                 }
41.             }
42.         }
43. 
44.         /// <summary>
45.         /// Converts a PhotoOrientation value into a human-readable string.
46.         /// The text is adapted from the EXIF specification.
47.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention,
48.         /// while the EXIF spec uses a clockwise convention.
49.         /// </summary>
50.         public static string GetOrientationString(PhotoOrientation input)
51.         {
52.             switch (input)
53.             {
54.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
55.                     return "No rotation";
56.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipHorizontal:
57.                     return "Flip horizontally";
58.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
59.                     return "Rotate 180˚ clockwise";
60.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipVertical:
61.                     return "Flip vertically";
62.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transpose:
63.                     return "Rotate 270˚ clockwise, then flip horizontally";
64.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
65.                     return "Rotate 90˚ clockwise";
66.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transverse:
67.                     return "Rotate 90˚ clockwise, then flip horizontally";
68.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
69.                     return "Rotate 270˚ clockwise";
70.                 case PhotoOrientation.Unspecified:
71.                 default:
72.                     return "Unspecified";
73.             }
74.         }
75. 
76.         /// <summary>
77.         /// Converts a Windows.Storage.FileProperties.PhotoOrientation value into a
78.         /// Windows.Graphics.Imaging.BitmapRotation value.
79.         /// For PhotoOrientation values reflecting a flip/mirroring operation, returns "None";
80.         /// therefore this is a potentially lossy transformation.
81.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention,
82.         /// while BitmapRotation uses a clockwise convention.
83.         /// </summary>
84.         public static BitmapRotation ConvertToBitmapRotation(PhotoOrientation input)
85.         {
86.             switch (input)
87.             {
88.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
89.                     return BitmapRotation.None;
90.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
91.                     return BitmapRotation.Clockwise90Degrees;
92.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
93.                     return BitmapRotation.Clockwise180Degrees;
94.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
95.                     return BitmapRotation.Clockwise270Degrees;
96.                 default:
97.                     // Ignore any flip/mirrored values.
98.                     return BitmapRotation.None;
99.             }
100.         }
101. 
102.         /// <summary>
103.         /// Converts a Windows.Graphics.Imaging.BitmapRotation value into a
104.         /// Windows.Storage.FileProperties.PhotoOrientation value.
105.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention,
106.         /// while BitmapRotation uses a clockwise convention.
107.         /// </summary>
108.         public static PhotoOrientation ConvertToPhotoOrientation(BitmapRotation input)
109.         {
110.             switch (input)
111.             {
112.                 case BitmapRotation.None:
113.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
114.                 case BitmapRotation.Clockwise90Degrees:
115.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
116.                 case BitmapRotation.Clockwise180Degrees:
117.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
118.                 case BitmapRotation.Clockwise270Degrees:
119.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
120.                 default:
121.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
122.             }
123.         }
124. 
125.         /// <summary>
126.         /// Converts an unsigned integer, corresponding to the EXIF orientation flag, to a
127.         /// Windows.Storage.FileProperties.PhotoOrientation value. Note that the actual
128.         /// PhotoOrientation enumeration values directly map to the EXIF orientation flag; 
129.         /// this method simply provides a typesafe means of converting between the two in C#,
130.         /// in addition to handling the Unspecified case.
131.         /// </summary>
132.         public static PhotoOrientation ConvertToPhotoOrientation(ushort input)
133.         {
134.             switch (input)
135.             {
136.                 case 1:
137.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
138.                 case 2:
139.                     return PhotoOrientation.FlipHorizontal;
140.                 case 3:
141.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
142.                 case 4:
143.                     return PhotoOrientation.FlipVertical;
144.                 case 5:
145.                     return PhotoOrientation.Transpose;
146.                 case 6:
147.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
148.                 case 7:
149.                     return PhotoOrientation.Transverse;
150.                 case 8:
151.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
152.                 default:
153.                     return PhotoOrientation.Unspecified;
154.             }
155.         }
156. 
157.         /// <summary>
158.         /// Counterpart to ConvertToPhotoOrientation(ushort input), maps PhotoOrientation enum
159.         /// values to an unsigned 16-bit integer representing the EXIF orientation flag.
160.         /// </summary>
161.         public static ushort ConvertToExifOrientationFlag(PhotoOrientation input)
162.         {
163.             switch (input)
164.             {
165.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
166.                     return 1;
167.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipHorizontal:
168.                     return 2;
169.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
170.                     return 3;
171.                 case PhotoOrientation.FlipVertical:
172.                     return 4;
173.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transpose:
174.                     return 5;
175.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
176.                     return 6;
177.                 case PhotoOrientation.Transverse:
178.                     return 7;
179.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
180.                     return 8;
181.                 default:
182.                     return 1;
183.             }
184.         }
185. 
186.         /// <summary>
187.         /// "Adds" two PhotoOrientation values. For simplicity, does not handle any values with
188.         /// flip/mirroring; therefore this is a potentially lossy transformation.
189.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention.
190.         /// </summary>
191.         public static PhotoOrientation AddPhotoOrientation(
192.             PhotoOrientation value1,
193.             PhotoOrientation value2
194.             )
195.         {
196.             switch (value2)
197.             {
198.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
199.                     return Add90DegreesCCW(value1);
200.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
201.                     return Add90DegreesCCW(Add90DegreesCCW(value1));
202.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
203.                     return Add90DegreesCW(value1);
204.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
205.                 default:
206.                     // Ignore any values with flip/mirroring.
207.                     return value1;
208.             }
209.         }
210. 
211.         /// <summary>
212.         /// "Add" 90 degrees clockwise rotation to a PhotoOrientation value.
213.         /// For simplicity, does not handle any values with flip/mirroring; therefore this can be
214.         /// a lossy transformation.
215.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention.
216.         /// </summary>
217.         public static PhotoOrientation Add90DegreesCW(PhotoOrientation input)
218.         {
219.             switch (input)
220.             {
221.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
222.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
223.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
224.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
225.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
226.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
227.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
228.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
229.                 default:
230.                     // Ignore any values with flip/mirroring.
231.                     return PhotoOrientation.Unspecified;
232.             }
233.         }
234. 
235.         /// <summary>
236.         /// "Add" 90 degrees counter-clockwise rotation to a PhotoOrientation value.
237.         /// For simplicity, does not handle any values with flip/mirroring; therefore this can be
238.         /// a lossy transformation.
239.         /// Note that PhotoOrientation uses a counterclockwise convention.
240.         /// </summary>
241.         public static PhotoOrientation Add90DegreesCCW(PhotoOrientation input)
242.         {
243.             switch (input)
244.             {
245.                 case PhotoOrientation.Normal:
246.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
247.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate90:
248.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
249.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate180:
250.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
251.                 case PhotoOrientation.Rotate270:
252.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
253.                 default:
254.                     // Ignore any values with flip/mirroring.
255.                     return PhotoOrientation.Unspecified;
256.             }
257.         }
258.     }
259. }
260.

131.         {

144.                 propertyText.Add("Orientation", m_localSettings["scenario1Orientation"].ToString());
145.                 propertyText.Add("LatDeg", m_localSettings["scenario1LatDeg"].ToString());
146.                 propertyText.Add("LatMin", m_localSettings["scenario1LatMin"].ToString());
147.                 propertyText.Add("LatSec", m_localSettings["scenario1LatSec"].ToString());
148.                 propertyText.Add("LatRef", m_localSettings["scenario1LatRef"].ToString());
149.                 propertyText.Add("LongDeg", m_localSettings["scenario1LongDeg"].ToString());
150.                 propertyText.Add("LongMin", m_localSettings["scenario1LongMin"].ToString());
151.                 propertyText.Add("LongSec", m_localSettings["scenario1LongSec"].ToString());

154.                 propertyText.Add("FNumber", m_localSettings["scenario1FNumber"].ToString());
155. 
156.                 await DisplayImageUIAsync(file, propertyText);
157.

159.                     "Loaded file from persisted state: " + file.Name,
160.                     NotifyType.StatusMessage
161.                     );
162. 
163.                 CloseButton.IsEnabled = true;
164.                 ApplyButton.IsEnabled = true;
165.             }
166.             catch (Exception err)

168.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Error: " + err.Message, NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
169.                 ResetSessionState();
170.                 ResetPersistedState();
171.             }
172.         }

174.         /// <summary>
175.         /// Load an image from a file and display some basic imaging properties.
176.         /// </summary>
177.         /// <param name="file">The image to load.</param>
178.         private async Task LoadFileAsync(StorageFile file)
179.         {
180.             try
181.             {
182.                 // Request persisted access permissions to the file the user selected.
183.                 // This allows the app to directly load the file in the future without relying on a
184.                 // broker such as the file picker.
185.                 m_fileToken = m_futureAccess.Add(file);
186. 
187.                 // Windows.Storage.FileProperties.ImageProperties provides convenience access to
188.                 // commonly-used properties such as geolocation and keywords. It also accepts
189.                 // queries for Windows property system keys such as "System.Photo.Aperture".
190.                 m_imageProperties = await file.Properties.GetImagePropertiesAsync();
191. 
192.                 string[] requests =
193.                 {
194.                     "System.Photo.ExposureTime",        // In seconds
195.                     "System.Photo.FNumber"              // F-stop values defined by EXIF spec
196.                 };
197. 
198.                 IDictionary<string, object> retrievedProps =
199.                     await m_imageProperties.RetrievePropertiesAsync(requests);
200.                 await DisplayImageUIAsync(file, GetImagePropertiesForDisplay(retrievedProps));
--- RESULT ---

101.                     );

103.                 m_localSettings.Add("scenario2ExifOrientation",
104.                     Helpers.ConvertToExifOrientationFlag(m_exifOrientation)
105.                     );
106. 
107.                 m_localSettings.Add("scenario2DisableExif", m_disableExifOrientation);
108. 
109.                 deferral.Complete();
110.             }

113.         /// <summary>
114.         /// Reads the file token and image transform variables from the persisted state and 
115.         /// restores the UI.
116.         /// </summary>
117.         private async void RestoreDataFromPersistedState()
118.         {
119.             try
120.             {
121.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Loading image file from persisted state...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
122. 
123.                 m_fileToken = (string)m_localSettings["scenario2FileToken"];
124.                 m_displayWidthNonScaled = (uint)m_localSettings["scenario2Width"];
125.                 m_displayHeightNonScaled = (uint)m_localSettings["scenario2Height"];
126.                 m_scaleFactor = (double)m_localSettings["scenario2Scale"];
127. 
128.                 PhotoOrientation desiredOrientation =
129.                     Helpers.ConvertToPhotoOrientation((ushort)m_localSettings["scenario2UserRotation"]);
130. 
131.                 m_exifOrientation =
132.                     Helpers.ConvertToPhotoOrientation((ushort)m_localSettings["scenario2ExifOrientation"]);
133. 
134.                 m_disableExifOrientation = (bool)m_localSettings["scenario2DisableExif"];
135. 
136.                 // Display the image in the UI.
137.                 StorageFile file = await m_futureAccess.GetFileAsync(m_fileToken);
138.                 BitmapImage src = new BitmapImage();
139. 
140.                 using (IRandomAccessStream stream = await file.OpenAsync(FileAccessMode.Read))
141.                 {
142.                     await src.SetSourceAsync(stream);
143.                 }
144.                 PreviewImage.Source = src;
145.                 AutomationProperties.SetName(PreviewImage, file.Name);
146. 
147.                 // Display the image dimensions and transformation state in the UI.
148.                 ExifOrientationTextblock.Text = Helpers.GetOrientationString(m_exifOrientation);
149.                 ScaleSlider.Value = m_scaleFactor * 100;
150.                 UpdateImageDimensionsUI();
151. 
152.                 // Restore the image tag's rotation transform.
153.                 while (desiredOrientation != m_userRotation)
154.                 {
155.                     RotateRight_Click(null, null);
156.                 }
157. 
158.                 RotateRightButton.IsEnabled = true;
159.                 RotateLeftButton.IsEnabled = true;
160.                 SaveButton.IsEnabled = true;
161.                 CloseButton.IsEnabled = true;
162.                 SaveAsButton.IsEnabled = true;
163.                 ScaleSlider.IsEnabled = true;
164.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Loaded image file from persisted state: " + file.Name, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
165.             }
166.             catch (Exception err)
167.             {
168.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Error: " + err.Message, NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
169.                 ResetSessionState();
170.                 ResetPersistedState();
171.             }
172.         }

<a id="startup-crashes"></a>
## Diagnosing Startup CrashesA packaged WinUI 3 app can build cleanly and still crash the instant it launches — `winapp run` returns no usable window and the process is gone. The build log and the analyzer cannot see this; only a real launch does. The **wrong** way to chase it is to sprinkle `File.WriteAllText` traces through `Program.cs` / `App.xaml.cs` and re-run repeatedly — that burns time and usually misses the real frame (the crash often happens in native activation, before your managed `Main` even runs).**Capture the real exception first.** Either run the launch-and-diagnose helper:```powershell
& "<skill-root>/scripts/Test-AppLaunch.ps1" -Target "<winui3-project-root>"
```…or read the Windows **Application** event log directly for the two events the crash writes:```powershell
Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 40 |
  Where-Object { $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) } |
  ForEach-Object { "`n=== Event $($_.Id) @ $($_.TimeCreated) ==="; $_.Message }
```- **Event 1000** — faulting module + native **Exception code** (e.g. `0x8001010E`).
- **Event 1026** — *.NET Runtime*: the managed **exception type, message, and stack**. This is the one that names the actual throwing frame. Read it before changing any code.**Known native codes and what they usually mean** (the code names the error *class*; the real fix is whatever the captured stack points at):| Exception code | Meaning | Usual migration cause → where to look |
|---|---|---|
| `0x80004003` | `E_POINTER` | Static-window **init-order race** — a `Page` read `App.MainWindow` (or another static window reference) before `OnLaunched` assigned it. Keep `MainWindow`'s constructor inert and navigate after `Activate`. See [Initialization order](#windowing). |
| `0x8001010E` | `RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD` | A **thread/apartment-affined object** was accessed during startup — commonly a view- or `CoreWindow`-affined UWP API touched from a `static` initializer, a type constructor, or off the UI thread. Construct/access it on the UI thread *after* `Activate`. If the API has no WinUI 3 desktop equivalent, defer it. |
| `0xE0434352` | Managed CLR exception | Read the **.NET exception type** in event 1026. `TypeLoadException` / `FileNotFoundException` almost always means a missing or version-incompatible package reference, not your code. |
| `0xC000027B` | Native stowed exception | Often a legacy projection/activation incompatibility for an API used at startup. If the API/contract is unsupported on the current OS, defer it. |> Do **not** assume the entry point is the problem. A custom `Program.Main` for WinUI 3 **correctly** carries `[STAThread]` + `ComWrappersSupport.InitializeComWrappers()` + the `DispatcherQueueSynchronizationContext` setup — this matches the SDK's auto-generated `Main`. `[STAThread]` is **required**, not a bug. If you have a hand-written entry point and don't need single-instancing/redirection, the simplest path is to delete it and let the SDK generate `Main`.The Step 4 validator runs this same check (`Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` Section 7) and **fails** when the app registers but dies at startup, surfacing the captured signature in `.validator-diagnostics.txt`.
<exited with exit code 0>

{"path":"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run218\\000_SimpleImaging\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Test-AppLaunch.ps1","view_range":[140,260]}

140. # Sanity: the layout needs an exe + a manifest, else winapp run can't launch it.
141. $exes = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $Layout -Filter '*.exe' -File -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
142. $exeNames = @($exes | ForEach-Object { [System.IO.Path]::GetFileNameWithoutExtension($_.Name) })
143. $hasMfst = (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppxManifest.xml')) -or
144.            (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Layout 'AppX\AppxManifest.xml'))
145. if ($exes.Count -eq 0 -or -not $hasMfst) {
146.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "Layout is incomplete (need an .exe + AppxManifest.xml): $Layout" @{ layout = $Layout })
147. }
148. if (-not (Get-Command winapp -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
149.     Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' "winapp CLI not on PATH - cannot launch the packaged app." @{ layout = $Layout })
150. }
151. 
152. # ---- Launch ------------------------------------------------------------------
153. $runRaw = & winapp run "$Layout" --detach --json 2>&1 | Out-String
154. $run = $null
155. try { $run = $runRaw.Trim() | ConvertFrom-Json -ErrorAction Stop } catch { }
156. $thePid = $null; $aumid = $null; $runErr = $null
157. if ($run) {
158.     foreach ($p in 'ProcessId','PID','Pid') { if ($run.$p) { $thePid = [int]$run.$p; break } }
159.     if ($run.AUMID) { $aumid = [string]$run.AUMID }
160.     if ($run.Error) { $runErr = [string]$run.Error }
161. }
162. 
163. # ---- WER capture helper ------------------------------------------------------
164. # Reads the real unhandled-exception signature for the just-crashed app:
165. #   event 1000  -> faulting module + native Exception code (e.g. 0x8001010E)
166. #   event 1026  -> .NET Runtime: managed exception TYPE + message + stack
167. # Match on the layout's exe base name(s) within a short recent window.
168. function Get-CrashSignature {
169.     param([string[]]$Names)
170.     $sig = [ordered]@{ code = $null; module = $null; managedType = $null; message = $null; hint = $null; anchor = $null }
171.     if (-not $Names -or $Names.Count -eq 0) { return $sig }
172.     $namePattern = ($Names | ForEach-Object { [regex]::Escape($_ + '.exe') }) -join '|'
173.     $events = Get-WinEvent -LogName Application -MaxEvents 80 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
174.         Where-Object { $_.TimeCreated -gt (Get-Date).AddMinutes(-3) -and $_.Id -in 1000,1026 -and $_.Message -match $namePattern }
175. 
176.     $e1000 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1000 } | Select-Object -First 1
177.     if ($e1000) {
178.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Faulting module name:\s*([^,]+)') { $sig.module = $matches[1].Trim() }
179.         if ($e1000.Message -match 'Exception code:\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)') { $sig.code = $matches[1].ToLower() }
180.     }
181.     $e1026 = $events | Where-Object { $_.Id -eq 1026 } | Select-Object -First 1
182.     if ($e1026) {
183.         # ".NET Runtime" event: "Exception Info: <Type>: <message>"
184.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception)') { $sig.managedType = $matches[1].Trim() }
185.         if ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*[A-Za-z0-9_.]+Exception[^\r\n:]*:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
186.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
187.         } elseif ($e1026.Message -match 'Exception Info:\s*([^\r\n]+)') {
188.             $sig.message = $matches[1].Trim()
189.         }
190.     }
191. 
192.     # Map the native exception code to a conservative hint + pattern anchor.
193.     # These name the error CLASS and point at the captured stack / the doc; they
194.     # do not assert a single fix, since the real frame is in the captured exception.
195.     switch ($sig.code) {
196.         '0x80004003' {
197.             $sig.hint   = "E_POINTER - most often the static-window init-order race: a Page read App.MainWindow (or another static window ref) before OnLaunched assigned it. Keep MainWindow's ctor inert; navigate after Activate."
198.             $sig.anchor = 'windowing'
199.         }
200.         '0x8001010e' {
201.             $sig.hint   = "RPC_E_WRONG_THREAD - a thread/apartment-affined object was accessed during startup (commonly a view/CoreWindow-affined UWP API touched from a static initializer or off the UI thread). Construct/access it on the UI thread after Activate; if the API is unsupported on WinUI 3 desktop, defer it."
202.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
203.         }
204.         '0xe0434352' {
205.             $sig.hint   = "Managed .NET exception - see the .NET exception type captured above (event 1026). Resolve that type (e.g. TypeLoad / FileNotFound usually means a missing or incompatible package reference)."
206.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
207.         }
208.         '0xc000027b' {
209.             $sig.hint   = "Native stowed exception - frequently a legacy projection/activation incompatibility. If a UWP API/contract used at startup is unsupported on this OS, defer it per MIGRATION-DEFERRED.md."
210.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
211.         }
212.         default {
213.             $sig.hint   = "Startup crash - read the captured exception (event 1026) to find the throwing frame."
214.             $sig.anchor = 'startup-crashes'
215.         }
216.     }
217.     return $sig
218. }
219. 
220. # ---- Classify ----------------------------------------------------------------
221. if ($thePid) {
222.     Start-Sleep -Seconds $SettleSeconds
223.     $alive = $null -ne (Get-Process -Id $thePid -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)
224.     if ($alive) {
225.         # Best-effort cleanup so we don't leave the dev-registered app running.
226.         try { Stop-Process -Id $thePid -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue } catch {}
227.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $true 'running' "App stayed alive ${SettleSeconds}s after launch (pid $thePid)." @{ layout = $Layout; pid = $thePid; aumid = $aumid })
228.     } else {
229.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
230.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' "App launched (pid $thePid) but exited within ${SettleSeconds}s - startup crash." @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
231.     }
232. } else {
233.     # No PID. Distinguish a startup crash from a deploy/env failure by whether the
234.     # app actually got REGISTERED/ACTIVATED (winapp emits an AUMID once it does).
235.     if ($aumid) {
236.         # Registered + activated, then threw before it could report a PID => startup crash (DEFECT).
237.         $crash = Get-CrashSignature -Names $exeNames
238.         $detail = "App registered (AUMID present) but crashed during activation/startup"
239.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - winapp reported '" + $runErr + "'" }
240.         $detail = $detail + "."
241.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'crashed' $detail @{ layout = $Layout; aumid = $aumid; crash = [pscustomobject]$crash })
242.     } else {
243.         # Never registered => deployment/environment problem, not a code defect. Inconclusive.
244.         $detail = "winapp run did not register the app (no AUMID, no PID)"
245.         if ($runErr) { $detail = $detail + " - '" + $runErr + "'" }
246.         $detail = $detail + ". Usually Developer Mode off, a missing framework dependency, cert issue, or MAX_PATH - not a migration defect."
247.         Write-LaunchOut (New-LaunchResult $false 'unavailable' $detail @{ layout = $Layout })
248.     }
249. }
250.
